STANDARD CITY AGREEMENT FOR SERVICES 
 
 
City Contract #   
 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
 
Furnishing and Installing a Parking Access, Permit and Revenue 
Control System 
 
 
THIS  AGREEMENT  is  made  and  entered into this     day of  
, 2018, by and between the 
City of Cheyenne (the “City”), located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Passport 
Labs, Inc. (the “Contractor”), located at 128 S. Tryon Street, Suite 128, Charlotte, NC 28202. 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and between the 
parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Contractor agrees to provide services and license all software, including all web and mobile 
applications and related documentation, necessary for the Contractor to operate (1) a custom- 
branded mobile payment for parking program and (2) a digital permits for parking system. 
 
2. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Contractor will 
invoice the City and the City agrees to pay the Contractor pursuant to the payment terms set forth in 
Exhibit A, which is attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference, the total amount of all 
such invoices not to exceed Five Hundred Seventy-Four Thousand and Two Dollars and Fifty-
Eight Cents ($574,002.58). 
 
No additional funding under this Agreement shall be expended absent the Parties executing a 
modification pursuant to the terms of this Agreement. Any work performed in excess of the amount 
provided for herein and absent an executed modification under these terms shall not entitle the 
Contractor to any additional compensation. 
 
Contractor may not utilize funds specified for use in support of one task, as identified in RFP-16- 
18, if applicable, in support of another identified task, as identified in the RFP, if applicable. If 
Contractor desires to transfer budgeted funds from one identified task to another, Contractor must 
obtain the written and signed consent of the City’s project manager for this project, the President 
of the City’s governing body or his or her designee, and the Mayor. 
 
Any work performed by the Contractor that utilizes funds identified for a different task without 
prior written execution of an instrument authorizing the transfer and demonstrating agreement by 
both Parties shall not be entitled to compensation. Electronic communications including, but not 
limited to email or text messages, do not constitute written instruments sufficient to authorize such 
modifications. Executed instruments authorizing such transfers may be transmittedelectronically. 
 
3. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 

2 
 
The Project shall be successfully completed in accordance with the Project schedule as contained 
within the RFP or as agreed upon between the parties to this Agreement. 
4. 
CONTRACTOR RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Contractor shall perform in a manner consistent 
with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same profession currently 
practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the same or similar locality. Upon 
notice to the Contractor and by agreement between the parties, the Contractor will, without 
additional compensation, correct those services not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City of Cheyenne has 
established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and drug usage by 
City Employees. All architects, engineers and other contractors under contract with City of 
Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-contractors, are required to comply with the provisions of 
the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property 
or other sites occupied by the Contractor while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Contractor to familiarize themselves with the requirements 
of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-contractors of the requirements and ensure 
their compliance. If the Contractor, their employees or sub-contractors are found in violation of this 
policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Contractor agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against any and 
all third-party liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses incident 
thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result of death or bodily 
injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on 
the environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused 
by (1) the Contractor’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or 
wrongful act, error or omission by the Contractor, or its employees or sub-contractors in the 
performance of this Agreement. The Contractor acknowledges that it may incur a financial 
obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. The Contractor shall not be 
responsible to indemnify the City to the extent of any liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, suits, 
costs, damages, losses, and expenses arising out of or resulting from the actions of the City or any 
third-party, and except as described above in this Section 7. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Contractor must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, as 
required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this Agreement. 

3 
 
Contractor shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or employer’s liability 
insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Contractor shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from any and 
all claims arising from the Contractor’s negligence in the performance of duties under this 
Agreement. The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided pursuant to an 
“occurrence” policy. 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. Contractor shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the City with 
a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect at the 
time the Contractor begins the provision of services under this Agreement; and 
b. In the event the Contractor’s services extend into a future policy period, the Contractor 
shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new certificate of insurance 
demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect during all periods of time that 
Contractor will provide services under this Agreement; and 
c. Contractor shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that Contractor has provided services under this Agreement; and 
d. In the event the Contractor or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior to the 
expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the 
Contractor shall provide to the City advance written notification of the termination of said 
coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years 
following the last date that Contractor has provided services under this Agreement. 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Contractor shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by endorsement 
on its insurance policies, and shall provide the City with a copy of the endorsements. This 
requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional liability insurance policies. 
Contractor shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above- 
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies 
required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement. It shall be an 
affirmative obligation upon Contractor to immediately notify in writing the city risk manager, city 
clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in 
the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, 
and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
The Contractor shall provide the City with copies of insurance policies and/or policy 
endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured. The City’s failure to request or 
review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s rights or Contractor’s 
obligation hereunder. 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 All 
parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, deniedthe 
benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award and performance of 
this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national origin or sex. 

4 
 
10. 
INDEPENDENT CONTRACTOR 
The Contractor shall function as an independent contractor for the purposes of this Agreement. The 
Contractor shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that may be incurred by the 
Contractor in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted 
as authorizing the Contractor or its agents or employees to act as an agent or representative of or 
on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
11. 
TAXES 
The Contractor agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts and 
obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
12. 
DEFAULT 
In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform according to the terms of this Agreement, 
such party may be declared in default. 
 
13. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, the non-defaulting party shall provide written 
notice to the defaulting party stating the alleged default and thedefaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days from the defaulting party’s receipt of the written notice within which to 
cure the default. In the event the default remains uncorrected after the cure period, the non- 
defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
a. Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed thecontract 
amount; or 
b. Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
14. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall be in effect until the Project has been successfully completed, including the 
expiration of any such warranty period, as is applicable, and such terms shall survive the completion 
of the project as are relevant to enforcement of the terms herein. 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Contractor, terminatethis 
Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon receipt of such notice, 
the Contractor shall: 
a. Discontinue all services affected; and 
b. Except as otherwise set forth herein, deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, 
drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and 
materials as may have been accumulated by the Contractor in performing this Agreement, 
whether completed or in process. 
c. In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Contractor for accepted 
work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such acceptance shall not be 
unreasonably withheld. 
 
15. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Contractor fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the City may 
elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Contractor. In the event of default of any of the 
conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to commence legal or 
equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its own costs and expenses, 
including without limitation, attorney’s fees. 

5 
 
16. 
WAIVER 
Failure by the City to specifically enforce any term shall not constitute waiver of such term. The 
waiver of any breach of any of the terms or conditions of this Agreement shall be limited to the act 
or acts constituting such breach, and shall never be construed as being a continuing or permanent 
waiver of any such terms or conditions, all of which shall be and remain in full force and effect as 
to future acts or happenings, notwithstanding any such individual waiver of any breach thereof. 
 
The City’s approval of drawings, plans, specifications, reports, and incidental work or materials 
furnished hereunder shall not in any way relieve the Contractor from responsibility for the technical 
accuracy of the work. The City’s approval or acceptance of, or payment for, any services shall not 
be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the City’s rights under this Agreement or any of its 
legal rights under statute and common law arising out of the performance of this Agreement. 
 
17. 
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS 
All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure to the benefit of and be binding upon the 
Parties hereto and their respective permitted successors and assigns. Neither Party shall assign this 
Agreement without prior written consent of both Parties; provided, however, that the Contractor 
may, without such written consent, but with ten (10) days’ written notice to Provider, assign this 
Agreement and its rights and delegate its obligations hereunder in connection with the transfer or 
sale of all or substantially all of its assets or business related to this Agreement, or in the event of 
its merger, consolidation, change in control or similar transaction. Any unpermitted assignment 
shall not operate to relieve either Party of its responsibilities hereunder. However, nothing in this 
Agreement shall prohibit the Contractor from subcontracting to a qualified subcontractor. 
 
18. 
THIRD-PARTY RIGHTS 
The Parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status of third party 
beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed to create such status. The rights, duties, and 
obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between the Parties to this Agreement, 
and shall inure solely to the benefit of the Parties to this Agreement. The Parties to this Agreement 
intend and expressly agree that only the Parties signatoryto this Agreement shall have any legal or 
equitable right to seek to enforce this Agreement, to seek any remedy arising out of a Party’s 
performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this Agreement, or to bring action for 
breach of this Agreement. This paragraph is not intended nor shall it be construed to waive any of 
the Parties’ immunities. 
 
19. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws 
of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any 
action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the venue shall be the First Judicial 
District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
20. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its governmental 
immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for the parties to pursue a 
contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the Agreement. Furthermore, the City 
specifically retain all immunities and defenses available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity 
pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations 
of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a 
waiver of governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall 

6 
 
not be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity. 
 
21. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are performed 
by the Contractor or its sub-contractors. This shall include, but not be limited to, the right to make 
site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts and consultants on site to 
examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to examine the books, ledgers, 
documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; and to observe personnel in every 
phase of performance of the related work. 
 
22. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Contractor shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment 
Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as 
amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly 
promulgated rules and regulations thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the 
grounds of age, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying 
disability in connection with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
23. 
PERSONNEL; LICENSES/PERMITS 
The Contractor agrees it will employ, at its own expense, all personnel necessary to perform the 
services required by this Agreement and in no event shall such personnel be the employees of the 
City. All services required shall be performed by the Contractorand personnel engaged therein shall 
be fully qualified under applicable federal, state and local law to undertake the work performed. 
Contractor assumes full and sole responsibility for the payment of all compensation and expenses 
of such personnel; for all applicable state and federal income tax, unemployment insurance, Social 
Security, disability insurance and other applicable withholdings, and the acquisition of any licenses 
and permits required to provide the services for the Project. 
24.

25. 
MODIFICATIONS 
Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to this Agreement which are mutually 
agreed upon by the Parties to this Agreement shall be incorporated by written instrument and 
executed by all Parties. No modification will be valid and binding but shall be void without a written 
instrument, executed by all Parties. 
 
Any work performed by the Contractor that exceeds the scope of the Agreement without prior 
written execution of an instrument authorizing the work and demonstrating agreement by both 
Parties shall not be entitled to any additional compensation. Electronic communications including, 
but not limited to email or text messages, do not constitute written instruments sufficient to 
authorize such modifications. Executed instruments authorizing such modifications may be 
transmitted electronically. 
 
26. 
SEVERABILITY 
If any provision of this Agreement is determined to be illegal or unenforceable for any reason, the 
same shall be severed from the Agreement and the remainder of the Agreement shall be given full 
force and effect. 
 
27. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of 11 pages (including the attached Exhibit A, consisting of 2 pages and

the event of conflict between or among the terms of the Agreement documents, the order of 
precedence shall  be the order of documents listed above, with the first-listed document having the

28. 
DATA RIGHTS 
This Section shall govern the rights of the Contractor and the City, as the case may be, withrespect 
to the data that is subject to this Agreement. Contractor will, by provisions in its Privacy Policy or 
otherwise, procure from such end users all such lawful consents and rights necessary to grant to the 
City the rights in such data as stated in this Section. Contractor’s Privacy Policy, as it may be 
amended from time to time in the Contractor’s sole discretion, can be viewed at 
https://passportinc.com/privacy-policy/. 
 
a. Operational data is data specific to the City’s operation that is provided by the City to the 
Contractor to be used in the providing of services. Operational data is specific to the City’s 
operation, which is not available to the Contractor publicly or by other means. Operational 
data may include, but is not limited to, zone information, rate information, operational 
schedules, business metrics, relevant details of partner agreements. In each case, 
Operational data may refer to past, present, or future states of such items. 
 
Operational data is the sole and exclusive property of the City. The City grants the 
Contractor a perpetual, irrevocable, royalty-free, non-exclusive, non-assignable, and non- 
transferrable license to Operational data, provided that, the Contractor may assign or 
transfer such license to a successor in connection with the transfer or sale of all or 
substantially all of its assets or business related to this agreement, or in the event of its 

8 
 
merger, consolidation, change in control or similar transaction. 
 
b. Payment Card Industry-Data Security Standard Information (“PCI-DSS 
Information”) consists of the following items, each as defined by the then-current Payment 
Card Industry Data Security Standards (“PCI-DSS”): Account Data; Cardholder Data; 
Primary Account Number; and Sensitive Authentication Data. 
 
The Contractor acquires a license or sublicense to the PCI-DSS Information from end 
users who share such data with the Contractor in connection with their use of the Software. 
The Contractor must secure such data in accordance with PCI-DSS. As such, the 
Contractor may not grant the City derivative rights to such PCI-DSS Information and the 
Contractor shall not be required to disclose such PCI-DSS Information to the City. 
 
c. Personal identifiable information (“PII”) is any representation of information that permits 
the identity of an individual to whom the information applies to be reasonably determined 
or inferred by either direct or indirect means. Name, address, social security number or 
other identifying number or code, telephone number, or email address directly identify 
individuals. Certain data elements—including gender, race, birth date, geographic 
indicator (such as zip code or postal code), and other descriptors—can be used in 
conjunction or with other data elements to indirectly identify individuals. Information 
permitting the physical or online contacting of a specific individual (e.g., IP address) is 
also personally identifiable information. 
 
End users of the Contractor’s Software own PII and license it to the Contractor pursuant 
to the Contractor’s Privacy Policy, as it may be amended from time to time in the 
Contractor’s sole discretion. The Contractor may sublicense PII to the City under certain 
conditions (including but not limited to the City’s compliance with information security 
controls and applicable regulations) that shall be memorialized separately if and when 
applicable. 
 
d. Activity data is any data generated in the providing of services under this agreement by 
the Contractor to the City and by end users’ interactions with the services or with the 
Contractor directly that is not otherwise PCI-DSS information or PII as defined above. 
Activity data may include, but is not limited to, user interaction data, geolocation data, 
opt-in/opt-out status (including compliance logs), purchase and session data, application 
diagnostic data, service performance data, and support data. Data that is derived from 
Activity data is also Activity data. 
 
Activity data is the sole and exclusive property of the Contractor. The Contractor grants 
the City an irrevocable, royalty-free, non-exclusive, non-assignable, and non-transferrable 
license to Activity data for the duration of the term of this Agreement and only to the 
extent and in the format that the Contractor chooses in its sole discretion to expose such 
data through its administrative portal or as otherwise agreed upon with the City and only 
for the City’s internal use in connection with the services provided under thisagreement.

32. 
CONFIDENTIALITY 
The City and the Contractor agree to treat this Agreement and all information furnished, or to be 
furnished, by or on behalf of the other party and information analyses, summaries and other work 
product derived from such information (collectively, the “Confidential Information”) in accordance 
with the provisions of this section and to take, or abstain from taking, all actions set forth herein. 
Each party, as a receiving party, will do the following things with regard to the Confidential 
Information of the other party: 
 
a. Prevent the disclosure of the Confidential Information by the receiving party and each of 
the receiving party’s employees, agents, and/or professionals to any third party other than 
as permitted under this Agreement; 
b. Use, and permit the use of, the Confidential Information only for the purposes of 
providing, or enjoying the benefit of, the goods, services, and/or software provided forin 
this Agreement (the “Purpose”); 
c. Disclose the Confidential Information only to such of the receiving party’s employees, 
agents, and professionals as have a bona fide need to possess or know the Confidential 
Information in the course of accomplishing, or advising the disclosing party with regard 
to, the Purpose; 
d. Cause each employee, agent, or professional to whom the receiving party discloses the 
Confidential Information to be bound by an obligation of confidentiality that is at least as 
rigorous as the obligations contained in this Agreement; and 
e. Return or destroy all written or other tangible copies of Confidential Information in the 
receiving party’s possession or direct or indirect control, including all extracts and copies 
thereof, within a reasonable time after, and in accordance with, the disclosing party’s 
request. 

10 
 
Nothing in this Agreement will prevent the receiving party from disclosing or using Confidential 
Information to the extent that: 
 
a. It is or becomes readily ascertainable by proper means by the public without any breach 
of a confidentiality obligation of the receiving party; 
b. It is received from a third party that is not under an obligation of confidentiality ofwhich 
the receiving party knew or had reason to know; 
c. It was independently developed by the receiving party without use of the Confidential 
Information; or 
d. It is required by law to be disclosed, provided that the receiving party provides to the 
disclosing party as much notice as is practicable under the circumstances of such 
requirement prior to disclosure and provides to the disclosing party, at the disclosing 
party’s expense, such reasonable assistance as the disclosing party requests in seeking 
confidential treatment, protective orders, nondisclosure, and/or similar measures. 
 
For the avoidance of doubt, none of the requirements of this Section shall prohibit the City from 
disclosing Confidential Information to the extent that such information is required to be disclosed 
pursuant to any open records law, open meetings law, or any other local public disclosure law 
applicable to the City.

genetec.com | Software and Hardware W arranty Levels 
3 
Overview 
 
 
The following document describes the hardware warranty levels and Hardware Extended W arranties 
offered by Genetec Inc. (“Genetec”) for products developed, manufactured and sold by Genetec. 
Standard Software Warranty Overview 
2.1 
Standard Software Warranty Coverage 
Genetec warrants that its software products will perform in all material respects in accordance with the 
accompanying user manual, and the media on which the Software Product resides will be free from 
defects in materials and workmanship under normal use. Software defects are covered through Service 
Releases and Cumulative Updates which are available for a period of 1 year from the date of the software 
purchase. A copy of the “Software License Agreement and GenetecTM Advantage Agreement” is also 
available for review on the Genetec™ website. http://www.genetec.com/Documents/EN/SLA/EN- 
Genetec-Software-License-Agreement-EULA.pdf 
2.2 
Products Covered by Standard Software Warranty 
 
All packaged software products developed by Genetec, including but not limited to Security Center, 
Security Center Mobile, Omnicast™, Synergis™, Plan Manager, Sipelia™ and AutoVu™ Patroller. 
Purchased upgrades are covered through the Standard W arranty. 
All custom software applications developed by Genetec using one of the Genetec™ Software 
Development Kits (SDK) for 90 days. 

genetec.com | Software and Hardware W arranty Levels 
4 
Standard Hardware Warranty Overview 
 
 
 
 
3.1 
Standard Hardware Warranty 
 
3.1.1 
Coverage 
Genetec warrants each product it manufactures to be free from defects in materials and assembly in the 
course of normal use and service, and provides technical assistance related to these defects. 
The Genetec™ Hardware Warranties cover both the product Hardware and Software running on the 
product, with the exception of appliances running one of Genetec’s software products (ex: SV-16, SV-32, 
SV-PRO) in which case the Software W arranty takes precedence over the hardware warranty for software 
related issues. 
The Genetec™ Standard Hardware W arranty does not cover labor to send a Genetec™ engineer on-site 
to evaluate a system problem and/or determine if there is a warranty issue or not and/or replace a 
defective product. 
3.1.2 
Products Covered 
All hardware products manufactured or resold by Genetec, including but not limited to, AutoVu™ Sharp, 
AutoVu™ SharpX, Access Control Hardware, SMC , Synergis™ Cloud Link, SV-16, SV-32, SV-PRO and 
respective accessories are covered by the Standard Warranty or falls under the manufacturer warranty.

genetec.com | Software and Hardware W arranty Levels 
6 
Genetec™ Hardware Warranty Terms and 
 
 
Conditions 
 
The Genetec™ Standard and/or Extended Hardware Warranty are governed by the below terms and 
conditions relating to repairs, replacements, remedies, or exclusions to the warranty. 
5.1.1 
Warranty on repairs and replacement parts 
All Genetec™ products serviced by Genetec for repairs and replacements parts are warranted against 
defects in workmanship and materials for a period of 90 days, or the remainder of the original warranty, 
whichever is the longest. 
5.1.2 
Exclusive Warranty Remedy 
During the applicable warranty period and in the event that a product is determined by Genetec to be 
defective in materials or assembly, Genetec will at its sole discretion either credit the customer the price 
paid for the defective product, or repair the defective product without charge, or replace the defective 
product with a new or refurbished product, or replace the defective product with a different product with 
identical or better specifications. 
5.1.3 
Warranty Exclusions 
The following items are not covered by the Genetec™ Standard Hardware W arranty: 
• 
Any equipment received by Genetec which does not match the Serial # captured during RMA 
process with GTAC. 
• 
Any equipment not furnished by Genetec. 
• 
A product which is used with non-supported ancillary equipment or software. 
• 
Freight cost to return a product to Genetec. 
• 
Defects or damage resulting from customer’s improper testing, operation, installation, 
maintenance, modification, alteration, or adjustment. 
• 
Defects or damage from misuse, accident or neglect. 
• 
Defects or damage resulting from use of the product in other than its normal and customary 
manner. 
• 
Defects or damage resulting from drilling holes, adding decals or other adhesives, or by 
painting the product. 
• 
Defects or damage due to lightning or other electrical discharge. 
• 
Product that is disassembled or repaired in such a manner as to adversely affect 
performance or prevent adequate inspection and testing to verify any warranty claim. 
• 
Modification or abuse of, or tampering with, the product. 
• 
Normal wear and tear. 
5.1.4 
Relocation of AutoVu™ Mobile Systems 
Relocating a hard mounted AutoVu™ Mobile System from one vehicle to another will void the warranty on 
the cables. This does not apply to portable systems with magnetic mounts.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_71202171.pdf
  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 11, 2018 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, 
and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. White, Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne, Agenda 
Item #30(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining 
Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 29, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”. 
  
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor 
license filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Mancuso’s LLC d/b/a Esther’s, 2114 Warren Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for proposed budgets 
for the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2019.  There being no 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes’ – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#4230) 

  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2018 and ending June 30, 2019. Stephanie 
Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA), spoke in support of the 
proposed 3% allocation affiliated with implementation of a merit based pay plan, citing the need 
for competitive wages, and commented in support of services provided by City employees.  She 
noted CPEA will be working with the administration on implementing the pay plan.  Susana 
Montana also spoke in favor of being able to reward employees through a merit increase program 
and encouraged support for the 3% evaluation plan.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by initiating a 3% merit-based pay increase for all non-uniformed employees allocated only 
after adoption and implementation of a merit-based pay increase plan to be taken from individual 
funds as appropriate from the general fund, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
the amendment by adding the following language after the word “plan” in the 3rd line: “as approved 
by the Governing Body”, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, 
confirmed no merit pay raises will be given until the pay for performance plan is approved by the 
governing body.  Discussion included: Time frame in which the Mayor anticipates submitting the 
plan to the governing body; patterning the plan upon the recent plan implemented by the Board of 
Public Utilities (BOPU) and sharing of resources on the performance plan between BOPU and the 
City; concern with approving funding for the plan prior to approval of the plan by the governing 
body; purpose of the plan to provide an evaluation system not just a monetary increase system, 
and implementation of the plan by December 31, 2018.  During discussion, Dr. Rinne raised a 
point of order that comments should be limited to the motion to amend the amendment on the floor 
with Mayor Orr ruling comments could continue.  During discussion, Darrin Hass, Deputy 
Director, Human Resources, stated compilation and implementation of the plan is on an aggressive 
schedule with an anticipated implementation date of December 31, 2018.  Motion to amend the 
amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Comment of clarification 
was made that not all employees may receive a 3% increase under the new plan.  Motion to amend, 
as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by initiating the on-going maintenance of the downtown trees in the amount of $110,000.00 
which includes one full-time arborist and a portion of a 45-foot aerial lift truck to be taken from 
5th penny sales tax fund overage collections, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Discussion topics 
included:  Concern with paying a salary position from 5th penny funds; new arborist position and 
affiliation with the ordinance amending the tree chapter in City Code also on the current agenda 
(Item #13), and removal of the position in the past budget.  Upon inquiry, Ron Downey, City 
Treasurer, stated 5th penny funds are an addition to the general fund and provided a brief 
explanation of future funding.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Johnson, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Dr. 
Rinne and Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by increasing the full-time employee cap 
number from 568 to 570 to include an additional arborist for the Forestry Division and a senior 
heavy equipment operator for the Compost Division, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed and included 
the following topics:  Supplemental department budget requests; deferred maintenance needs; 

  
funding amount of $250,000.00 contained in the proposed Public Works budget for on-going 
maintenance needs, and keeping the former “model” of a budget line item for capital improvements 
and deferred maintenance in the general fund.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Vicki 
Nemecek, Director of Public Works, confirmed solid waste department employees were included 
in the 570 employee count; Mr. Downey advised any monies left in the current capital 
improvements fund budget would be transferred to reserves at the end of the current fiscal year 
but could be reallocated through re-appropriation, and Mayor Orr provided information generally 
on the budget and proposed funding of $250,000.00 as a start in addressing maintenance needs.  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by moving $250,000.00 from the facilities maintenance line item 
contained in the proposed Public Works budget; re-create the capital improvement line item for 
the FY2019 budget and carry over any remaining balance in that line item from this fiscal year 
into next year’s budget, and include the $250,000.00 in the FY2019 capital improvement line item 
for use toward deferred maintenance and identified needs affiliated with departmental FY2019 
supplemental budget requests, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mayor Orr advised the funding was 
placed in the Public Works budget as that department oversees facilities, which has the greatest 
maintenance needs.  Discussion included:  Average amount of funding in the capital improvement 
line item; retaining $78,000 in the Public Works facilities budget for payment of annual 
maintenance requirements and other necessities for facilities; remaining funds in the capital 
improvement line item for the current budget; need for the state legislature to reform the tax system 
to allow for more stable funding sources for municipalities to address infrastructure needs; 
prioritizing maintenance needs for use of the funding; current Capital Improvement Plan (CIP), 
and providing the Mayor and department directors flexibility in requesting real-time 
recommendations for use of the money as opposed to “locking into” the CIP.  During discussion 
and upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised the $250,000.00 was an additional request to the budget 
and that $78,000.00 should remain in the facilities maintenance line item to pay for annual 
maintenance responsibilities and other facilities maintenance necessities.  Mr. Downey provided 
information on the amount of funding in the current budget for capital improvements 
($820,000.00) that would go into reserves but could be re-appropriated and the amount of 
$328,765.00 for capital improvements in the proposed FY2019 budget.  Mayor Orr commented on 
the lack of adoption of the CIP by the governing body.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
The following is a summary of the proposed budget, including the aforementioned amendments: 
General Fund, $53,750,797; Weed and Pest, $583,147; Youth Alternatives Grants, $635,008; 
Development Impact Fees, $151,200; Recreation Programs, $1,159,555; Community 
Development Block Grant, $398,422; Law Enforcement Grants, $221,584; Transportation 
Planning, $870,097; FTA Transit Grant, $2,447,162; Juvenile Justice, $73,037; Special Friends, 
$77,394; Specific Purpose Option Tax, One Percent Sales Tax 2015-18, $11,185,325; Youth 
Activities/Ropes, $18,000; Golf Facilities, $201,000; Fleet Maintenance, $3,949,084; Solid Waste 
Management, $16,573,784; Civic Center, $2,177,792; Ice & Events Center, $644,760; Perpetual 
Care, $2,000; Total City, $95,119,148; Water Enterprise, $24,070,900; Sewer Enterprise, 
$13,081,450; Total Appropriation, $132,271,498. (#4231)

ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Repealing in its entirety Chapter 12.16, Trees and 
Shrubs, and creating a new Chapter 12.16, Trees, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Discussion topics included:  Past maintenance responsibility for care 
of trees in the downtown area; tree replacement costs; expertise that is needed involving 
maintenance knowledge and diseased and dangerous trees; funding of a new staff position in Urban 
Forestry affiliated with the ordinance; Urban Forestry guidelines, purpose and promulgation (i.e. 
Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act) of same; City forestry staff services on public and private 
property; use of revenue from fees that are generated; improved notification process to property 
owners; local arborists’ knowledge of the proposed ordinance, and simplified provisions and 
additional flexibility provided through the proposed ordinance.  During discussion, Alessandra 
McCoy Fakelman, Assistant City Attorney, and Mark Ellison, City Forester, provided information 
in response to inquiries.  Motion to approve carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Shanor voting “no”.

Consideration of RFP-16-18 for a contract for furnishing and installing a parking access, 
permit and revenue control system.  Captain Nathan Buseck, Police Department, reviewed the 
project’s history, including reorganization of the parking patrol staff, parking structure permits and 
parking enforcement which are now a part of Police Department operations.  Captain Buseck 
provided information on proposed improvements and processes through implementation of the 
proposed new system, citing new technologies that will be incorporated; Parking Transition Team; 
public communications on the proposed system; RFP responses and elements of the recommended 
proposal to be accepted that will provide better enforcement and revenue management and more 
user convenience to customers; anticipated contract costs; revenue assistance for paying parking 
structure revenue bonds, and use of license plate reader cameras and affiliated software.  Vicki 
Nemecek spoke in support of the new system; Astrid, owner of the Plains Hotel, expressed 
concerns about parking in the downtown area, and Thomas Reddington expressed his belief that 
free parking should be considered.  Upon inquiries by governing body members, Captain Buseck 
responded with additional information on current parking enforcement and parking permit 
procedures, clarifying that the fee structure was not changing, and components of the proposed 
new updated system involving technology.  Mr. White moved to approve (proposal from Passport 
Labs, Inc., Charlotte, North Carolina) in an amount not to exceed $574,002.58, seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Discussion included system costs vs. unknown revenue amounts that may be generated 
through use of the new system; current manual “chalking” that is conducted for enforcing overtime 
parking violators; purpose of the new system, and postponing the item to receive additional 
information.  Mr. Case moved to postpone to June 25, 2018, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. 
Reddington inquired about public comment opportunities if the item were postponed with Mayor 
Orr advising of the dates and times of the next Finance Committee and City Council meeting.  
Upon inquiry, Logan Sharpe, City Attorney’s office, advised a postponement would not affect 
provisions of the RFP and affiliated contract.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Mr. Roybal, 
Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr. 
 

  
[CA]

the City of Cheyenne through the Information Technology Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) -- 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-29-18 for furnishing 18 police vehicles with equipment installed

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-26-18 for furnishing one new 2019 cab and chassis, 4x4 with

referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointment of Frank Gerstenkorn to the City of Cheyenne Personnel Commission.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral appointee). 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, June 25, 2018 at 6:00 
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Stults Management LLC d/b/a The 
Office Bar and Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by 
Robert Moberly d/b/a Bob’s Place, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd.). 
 
 
Announcement was made that two Calcuttas, benefitting Cheyenne area charities, will be 
held as follows:  Thursday, July 19, 2018 beginning at 7:00 p.m. at the Redwood Lounge, 2105 
East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the 2018 Cheyenne Frontier Days 
Wild Horse Races, and Friday, July 27, 2018 beginning at 5:30 p.m. in the Headquarters tent 
located in front of the Old West Museum, 4601 North Carey Avenue, Frontier Park, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (10% of proceeds to bona fide charities). 
 
 
Under other business:  
 
 
Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation for efforts made for budget planning sessions with 
staff in preparation of the FY2019 budget.  Mayor Orr thanked City Council members for their 
participation in the additional budget work sessions.   
 
Mr. Johnson expressed appreciation to those who attended the fundraiser at Danielsmark 
for the Depot Splash Pad Project, and Mr. Case noted approximately $2,000 has been raised so far 
in private funding toward the project. 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on issues current, as well as past, governing bodies and 
administrations face regarding the economy, budgets and municipal funding; community issues, 
and decision making, and thanked co-governing body members and City staff for their efforts. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:58 p.m. 
 
 
 

  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 23, 2018 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Scott Roybal.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City 
Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 9, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mr. Johnson moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on

the governing body present. (#4222)

Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium-Density for Lot 14, Block 5, Replat of

present.  

ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of Lot 1, Block 1, Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and portions of the east half of Section 25, 
T.14N., R.66W., of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including

Road).  John Edwards provided a handout and reviewed development history of Saddle Ridge 
Subdivision and development plans proposed for the subdivision’s 13th Filing involving 
annexation and re-zoning of the property.  Mr. Edwards noted the types of residential housing that 
would be provided in addition to an area reserved for a new school and open space.  Mr. Shanor 
moved to approve on second reading with revised annexation map, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County A-1, Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MR, Medium-density Residential and P-Public for a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 
Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and portions of the east half of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., 
of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including Pershing Boulevard, 
east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sage Road, east of Gunsmoke Road).  Mr. Shanor 
moved to approve on second reading with revised map, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. Shanor moved 
to amend by substitute dated April 11, 2018, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Upon inquiry, Susana 
Montana, Planning and Development Department, explained the revised map includes a title 
clarification, and the substitute adds the zoning type “P-Public”, both being clerical adjustments. 
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from Conditional MUR,

Wellness Center (located at 510 West 29th Street, between O’Neil and Thomes Avenues).  Mr. 
Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending, creating, or deleting various chapters and 
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to 
adoption of the National Electrical Code, 2017 Edition.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry regarding stakeholder input, Robert Briggs, 
Planning and Development Director, explained Building department staff is compiling an email 
list of electrical contractors in the community to be contacted for their input. Mr. Briggs explained 
the electrical code is a standard adopted at the State level, which the City is required to adopt.  
Comment was made on initiating communication with local electrical contractors to receive 
feedback on any concerns.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.

Mustangs baseball; commented on financial concerns and responsibilities related to last year’s 
tournament, and scheduling of ball fields, and encouraged the governing body to “do the right 
thing” for Cheyenne and local baseball players.  Major Dorr spoke in support of Mr. Avila 
reimbursing the City and paying his bills incurred from last year’s tournament prior to allowing 
Mr. Avila use of the ball fields for the 2018 tournament.  During public comments, Mayor Orr 
clarified last year’s financial arrangement was between the City and Cheyenne Mustangs, not 
directly with Mr. Avila.  Mr. White reported there was no recommendation from Finance 
Committee on the item.  Mr. Johnson moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Shanor moved 
to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Case.  It was explained the amendment will place certain 
conditions that must be fulfilled by Mr. Avila in order to hold the 2018 tournament.  Discussion 
included the following:  Changes contained within the proposed substitute; time frame required to 
submit revenue documentation and payment to the City of 30% of gross revenues from the 
tournament; payment for field use; use of home dugout for participating Cheyenne teams; whether 
the 7th Whereas clause contained within the resolution was needed, and the proposed substitute 
not being available to Council members or the public prior to the current meeting.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Shanor advised the proposed amendment had been shared with Cheyenne Mustangs but had 
not been reviewed or approved by the City Attorney.  Jay Folds, Assistant Coach, Cheyenne 
Mustangs, spoke in support of the proposed substitute and the 2018 tournament.  Rob Jarosh, Hirst 
and Applegate law firm and supporter of Cheyenne Mustangs, stated he had received inquiries 
from City Council members relative to the City renting the fields to Mr. Avila, and explained his 
position that the City could rent the fields for purposes in the best interests of the City and for 
economic interests of the community, and spoke in support of the proposed substitute.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor 
and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr.  Mr. Shanor moved to 
amend as follows, seconded by Dr. Rinne: Under the Now Therefore, Be It Resolved clause, in 
Section 1 (d) and (e) change the phrase “within five days” to instead read “within thirty days”.  
Discussion included payment of certain team registration fees by a private donor, and whether the 
resolution should reference Mr. Avila only instead of Mr. Avila and SAA Enterprises, LLC, 

collectively.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, advised that SAA 
Enterprises, LLC, had been administratively dissolved and suggested the resolution be changed to 
include Steve Avila’s name only.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order noting the amendment on the 
floor should be addressed prior to any additional proposed amendments.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson and 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Discussion followed on reasons for naming Mr. Avila and SAA 
Enterprises, LLC, collectively in the 1st Whereas clause, and timing of field reservations for the 
2018 tournament through City staff.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by striking the 7th Whereas 
clause, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Jason Sanchez, Assistant Director, Community 
Recreation and Events Department, provided information on when the ball fields had been “put on 
hold” vs. Mr. Avila’s application to reserve the fields; inquiry for field reservation by the 
Mustangs, and standard staff policy on holding fields for tournaments.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. White and Mayor Orr.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne.  Comments of concern were made regarding the 
message to the community the substitute resolution relayed, and constructing amendments from 
the dais during meetings without advance notice.  Main motion, as amended three times, carried.  
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting 
“no” – Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mayor Orr. (#5927) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Rebecca Velez for 
concessionaire services at the Converse Softball Complex, the Brimmer Softball Complex, the 
Cheyenne Junior League Baseball Complex and the Powers Field Baseball Complex (revenue to 
City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6822) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Youth 
Baseball for concessionaire services at the Dutcher Baseball Complex (revenue to City).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6823) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Chicago Dog House 
for concessionaire services at the Pioneer Park Baseball Complex (revenue to City).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6824) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County 
School District Number One to provide drainage improvements on Sunflower Road adjacent to 
Buffalo Ridge Elementary School (Ward II Contingency and Engineering Drainage Funds).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $60,000). (#6825) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Green, LLC, for 
vegetation management along Dry Creek and Crow Creek (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning 
Organization, and AVI p.c., for the Parsley Boulevard Corridor Plan (MPO Planning funds) – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for an ATM machine 
at the Cheyenne Ice and Events Center (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Amazing Amusements for game machines 
at the Cheyenne Ice and Events Center (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Linda M. Moran, Gail L. 
Anderson and Vici Rae Foster, for real property to be used for the Christensen Road Project (1% 
Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Sung Thik Lim, for real 
property located at 308 West 17th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be used as the site for the 
construction of a municipal courthouse (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract for group health insurance for City of Cheyenne employees – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Delta Dental for group dental benefits for City 
employees – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification No. 3 to City Contract No. 6498 between the City of Cheyenne and 
DHM Design Corporation, Denver, CO, for professional services associated with the Civic Center 
Commons Project (FEMA Pre-Disaster Mitigation Grant and Wyoming Business Council Park 
Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $150,544.50). (#6498) 
 
[CA] 
Amendment No. 4 to Contract No. 5930 between the City of Cheyenne and KONE, Inc., 
Englewood, Colorado, to standardize pricing for elevator maintenance services and to add services 
for an elevator at the Botanic Gardens and Transfer Station – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Amendment No. 1 to Contract No. 5939 between the City of Cheyenne and Lamar 
Companies, Baton Rouge, Louisiana, to extend the term of lease for placement of billboards on 
the Belvoir Ranch (revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 6743, Professional Services Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne and River Oaks Communications Corporation, Centennial, 
Colorado, for drafting and negotiation of Charter Communications Cable Franchise renewal and 
CenturyLink Cable Television franchise agreements – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-18-18 for furnishing one new 2017-2018 front end wheel loader 
for the Street and Alley Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-20-18 for furnishing front end load containers for the Sanitation 
Division (Solid Waste Management funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Consideration to purchase six Self Contained Breathing Apparatuses (SCBA) and affiliated 
equipment from L.N. Curtis & Sons, Salt Lake City, UT, for the Cheyenne Fire and Rescue 
Department (Homeland Security funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to 
exceed $62,571.30). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase twenty 20 premium ice bumper cars from IBC International, 
Steamboat Springs, CO, for the Community Recreation and Events Department, Ice and Events 
Center (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed 
$148,700.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase Motorola complete vehicle 2-way radio systems and chargers 
from Wireless Advanced Communications, Evans, Colorado, for the Cheyenne Police Department 
(Homeland Security funds, 2015-2018 1% Sales Tax and 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) 
-- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
Appointment of Kyle Wall and Lane Pilbin to the International Fire Code Board of 
Appeals, and Steven Bates and David Halley to the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities. 
Dr. Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointees).   
 
Re-appointment of Kevin Williams to the International Fire Code Board of Appeals.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (Mayoral 
appointee). 
 
 
Under other business:  
 
 
Major Dorr spoke in opposition to people owing money to the City and not paying their 
debts. 
 
 
Mr. White commented on the success, attendance-wise, of the most recent Fridays in the 
Asher building concert series, and volunteers who are helping with the concerts. 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on the success of a fundraiser benefitting the Rooted in Cheyenne 
tree program which involved partnership with the Accomplice Beer Company and a special craft 
beer brewed for the fundraiser.  He noted another fundraiser, again in partnership with Accomplice, 
is being held in conjunction with Arbor Day. 
 
 
Mayor Orr advised the Bloomberg team will be in Cheyenne on April 24th, and the City 
will be introducing its Buildings with a Purpose Project during an event to solicit stakeholders and 
public input to be held April 24th at 1601 Central Ave. at 7:00 p.m. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:17 p.m. 
 
 
 
 

 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Carol Intlekofer, MMC 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
May 4, 2018

DESCRIPTION:  
Petition to annex 74,198 square feet into the City for residential development. 
 
LOCATION: 
1. Legal Description: Lot 14, Block 5, Replat Homestead Addition, First Filing, 1st 
Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
2. Location: Located south of Homestead Avenue, north of the Cheyenne Greenway 
3. Address: Unaddressed.

PROPOSED ZONING: City NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density

Lot 14, Block 5, Replat Homestead Addition, First Filing Annexation 
UDC-18-00069 
 
P a g e | 3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
The applicants have proposed annexing the subject property, Lot 14, Block 5, Replat Homestead 
Addition, First Filing, for residential development purposes. The developer intends to place small 
detached dwellings on the land, which would front onto the Cheyenne Greenway, which is directly south 
of the property. 
 
The property is surrounded by annexed land and is a true county pocket. This application is consistent 
with PlanCheyenne and is supported by the intent of the subject land’s Future Land Use Map designation 
and several of PlanCheyenne’s general policies. This annexation meets the review criteria found in State 
statute as well. The applicants have also submitted a zoning map amendment application for this same 
land to give the land the City zoning district of NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming State 15-1-402, staff recommends 
conditional approval of this annexation with two (2) conditions: 
 
1. Correct the title block to make the annexation recordable, per comments by Planning and 
Development Staff. 
2. Survey tie to one boundary course (2 corners) and coordinates from two (2) City Control 
Monuments, using State Plane Coordinates, per comments by the City Surveyor.

PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
This annexation is consistent with PlanCheyenne. The 
subject property is within the Urban Service Boundary 
(USB) and, per Policy 1.2.B, is encouraged to develop 
with an urban character. Policy 1.2.A explains that the 
USB is meant to guide urban growth and distinguish 
between urban and rural places. 
The land is also a true county pocket, as it is 100% 
surrounded by land that is already annexed into city 
limits. Therefore, Policy 1.4.B. of PlanCheyenne is 
supportive of this annexation, as it supports revitalization 
of a county pocket and underutilized land. 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the 
subject land has been designated as Urban Residential. 
The Urban Residential land use designation includes 
residential uses as a primary use and includes a wide 
variety of supporting and complementary uses as 
secondary uses. The applicant is proposing to develop this 
property with residential uses, which is congruent with 
PlanCheyenne.

ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the 
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)): 
 
COMMON WELFARE – An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare of 
the persons residing in the area and in the city or town. 
This annexation would allow the property to access City water, sanitary sewer, and sanitation service. 
City police and fire services would be available immediately, which serves the public safety and welfare. 
As this annexation would allow the property to connect to water and sewer services (after extensions), it 
would enhance the environmental quality of the land. Therefore, the annexation of this property would 
increase the health, safety, and welfare of the residents of the Cheyenne area. 
 
PART OF THE CITY – The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would be constituted a 
natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town. 
This property would constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the City. The contiguity 
along all boundaries of the property make the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The people 
who will live on this land are a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne. 
 
LOGICAL EXPANSION – The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing 
city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City services 
normally available to property owners would also be available to this location.  
 
CONTIGUOUS – The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or 
town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. 
The parcel proposed for annexation is 100% contiguous to City limits. 
 
UTILITIES – If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue franchises as necessary to service the annexed area pursuant to W.S. 15-1-4102. 
Electric utility franchises are in place to serve the parcels. 
 
CERTIFIED MAILING – The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected 
public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date, and location of the public hearing required by 
W.S. 15-1-405(a). 
A certified mailing was required to be made to property owners and agencies by 3/9/2018. Staff 
sent this certified mailing on 3/8/2018. 

Lot 14, Block 5, Replat Homestead Addition, First Filing Annexation 
UDC-18-00069 
 
P a g e | 9 
ANNEXATION REPORT DATA: In compliance with Wyoming State Statute 15-1-402 (c) the following 
information is herein provided: 
(i) 
A map is not required for this report based on the absence of exercising the authority 
granted under W.S. 15-3-202 (b) (ii). 
(ii) 
The total estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the landowners by the 
City related to the annexation of this vacant land is approximately $0.00.  Additional on- and 
off-site improvements may be required with subsequent development actions. 
(iii) 
Services customarily available to City residents include water, sewer, sanitation, police and 
fire protection. Police and Fire services will be provided immediately upon the recording of 
the annexation. Water and sewer services are provided upon application and payment to the 
Board of Public Utilities, with sanitation services occurring concurrently. Provision of these 
services is initiated by the property owner. Development will require extension of water and 
sewer services, which are available to the subject property.  
(iv) 
Fees for water, sewer, and sanitation services are a variable amount for residential units, 
based upon volume and meter size. Service costs will not be charged until development 
occurs. 
(v) 
The current property tax mill levy imposed is 67.17 and projected property tax mill levy 
imposed by the city is 72.17. 
(vi) 
The infrastructure described in (ii) above includes improvements required within the existing 
boundaries of the municipality to accommodate the proposed annexation. 
 
REVIEW 
Board of Public Utilities-BOPU: Board of Public Utilities, Linda Gunter 
As per sections 1.16.040, 1.16.050 and 1.16.060 of Chapter 1.16 of the Cheyenne City Code, it is the 
responsibility of the property Owner/Developer to extend the water and sanitary sewer utilities to and 
through the boundaries of the property to be served. 
The property Owner/Developer shall be required to extend the water and sanitary sewer utilities to the 
farthest point (or points) of the property being developed. These utility extensions shall be to a point (or 
points) that are logical for projected future connections by future development projects, as determined 
by the BOPU and the property Owner/Developer.  
All related work and activities associated with the construction of or modifications of the City’s water and 
sanitary sewer systems shall follow the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities Construction 
Standards and Specifications and the BOPU’s Infrastructure Policies and Design Criteria. P&D STAFF 
COMMENT: Provided as FYI 
 
City Surveyor: Engineering Department, Jack Studley 
1. Boundary data and legal descriptions appear in order. P&D STAFF COMMENT: Provided as FYI 
2. Survey tie to one boundary course (2 corners) and coordinates from two (2) City Control Monuments,
